5 . The workmaz is found to have worked in the Canteon 
for 66 days during September , 1982 to April, 1983 as Can 
teen Boy. The Canteen being not under the mapagement of 
tho State Bank of India , those dayy cannot be counted as 
the working period under the management of State Bank of 
Iudia . S .ace he was on daily wages, he had no legal status 
to continue in the cmployment of the Bank and he was 
accordingly put off the employment. 

6 . Howevor , in terms of the Settlement rcached with the 
Federation dated 15- 2 - 1985 the workinan was given a chance 
to face a regular selection . He appeared before the Seloc 
tion Bourd on 28 - 4 - 1986 but failed in the interview and 
ho was accordingly adviscd . Thus his services could not 
be continued and came to an end, 

7. The management has denied that the workman was 
employed or had been performing the duties of Messenger 
cum - Furrash with effect from 3 -5 - 1982 and continued till 
23 - 1 - 1983 . He was kept on Rs. 5 per day on Jaily wages 
for khas tatties purpose and during May to July , 1982 hc 
worked as khas tatti wala . He never worked as Messenger 
during the aforesaid period. The workman did not at all 
work in January , 1983. Ho however worked for 10 days 
in Februray and for 13 days in March , 1983. He was given 
an opportunity for regular employment and was given a 
chance to appear before the Selection Board but he did not 
succeed and therefore it cannot be said that he was pot 
given an opportunity for employment. The nanngedient 
did not violato the terms of settlement. It has been denied 
that he was deliborately denied the work , Management was 
free to discontinue his services because he was a daily 
wager The contrast therefore commences in the morning 
and terminates in the evening. No provisions of law have 
been violated , lle has not completed 240 days of continu 
Ous service at any time and is not entitled to the protection 
under the ID . Act. The reference is, therefore , liable to be 
rejectod. 

8 . The manageinent has examined himmself in support of 
its case while the workman has cxamined himself. 

9 . It may be pointed out that according to the workman 
he worked tipto 23- 1 - 1983 while according to the manage 
ment the workman worked from Fobruary , 1982 to April, 
1983 as detailed in the abovo Schedule . 

10 . It is true that though the workman has denied that he 
was a daily wage carner but from his own statement it can be 
Very well gathered that he was employed on daily wages 
from time to time. There is no evidence on record to 
show that his services were deliberately discontinued by the 
management to defeat the provisions of law . Thus it can 
not be said that he was legally dlacontinued from service .

In angwer to the charges he has submitted an explanation 
on 23 - 12 - 83 . This was not satisfactory to the Management 
and thercfore an enquiry was decided to be conducted into 
the matter , and an enquiry officer was appointed . The 
enquiry officer has conducted the enquiry in compliance 
with the principles of natural justice . In the enquiry the 
employee admitted the 1st, 4th and 5th charges. The en 
quiry officer has submitted a report finding that the em 
ployee is guilty of all charges except the third charge . 
Accepting the report of thc enquiry officer , the workman 
was dismissed from service . In any case the Court comes to 
a conclusion that thc enquiry was dcfective, the Manage 
ment may be permitted to substantiate the charges by adduc 
ing fresh evidence . The enquiry report is not perverso in 
any manner . The gravity of the proved misconducts de 
served the punishment of dismissal. An award may be 
passed in favour of the Management

oficer is not legal and proper . It follows that the findings

8 . The Management in its counter statement has made 
a request that they should be permitted to substantiate tho 
charges by adducing fresh evidence before this court in case 
tho coquiry is found to be defective by the Court. So the 
Management is allowed to adduce fresh evidence before this 
court to substantiate the charges against the workman , en 
pecially charge Nos. 2 , 3 and 4 . 

9. In the result it is hereby found that there was no 
proper and valid domestic enquiry and that the findings 
of the enquiry officer are also invalid ." 

II. The Management had attempted to substantiatc ifs 
case by fresh evidence before this court after the above 
udur . Onc witness was elamined as MW - 2 in that ilttempt 
and Exts. M - 2 and M - 3 were also marked . 

Ty . The Manager (Personnel), Banking Division in Calicut 
during 1979 to 1983 was examined As MW -2 . Ho would 
depose as follows 
Vijayachandran was clerk during the period 1979 Q 

1983. He issued a cheque to Haridag to discharge 
his personnal liabilities . The cheques were dis 
counted and money WAS received by Haridas on 
11 - 1 - 1981 And 18 - 3 - 1981. After issuing the cheques 
Vijayachandran has not followed up the fate of the 
chequos. Transaction relating to 16 - 3 -81 was cn 
tered on page 24 of Schedule No. 6 register . The 
13th item on 6 - 3 - 1981 was deleted under authenti 
cation of Vijayachandran , the Head Clerk . When an 
entry is cut and deleted the usual procedure is to 
make brief remarks against the particular entry and 
it should be brought to the notice of the Branchi 
Manager. Here no remarks have been recorded 
and this cannot be returned to the drawer but it 
should be returned only through the originating 
Branch . Chandrasekharan was the Branch Manager 
during the relevant seriod. He is in servico now . 
He is presently the Chief Inspector in the Training 
Centre. In the cross- cxamination he would depose 
that the allegation against the workman was that he 
issued two chcques to another employee and at Tirur 
Branch he discounted the cheques , Haridas is the 
primary responsible and drawer is also responsible . 
The amount covered by the two cheques were sub e 
quently remitted in the Bank and he was not aware 
as to whether the two cheques had reached in the 
Calicut Branch in due course . 


4 . The workman has filed a rejoinder reiterating his 
claims in the claim statement and refuting the contentions 
in the counter - statement filed by the Management . 


5. The points that arise for consideration arc whether 
the enquiry conducted by the enquiry officer is legel and 
proper and whether the findings entered into by the enquiry 
officer are supported by legal evidence . 


6 . The delinquent was an enployee in the management 
Bank , While he was working as Head Clerk of Parappadangadi 
Branch , Calicut of the State Bank of India a memo of 
charge was served on him calling for his explanation and 
he submitted his explanation . Not satisfied with the ex 
planation submitted by the workman , the Management 
ordered a domestic enquiry by appointing an enquiry officer ,

7 . The workman relies on 9 .0 . No. 26 ( V ) and 29(6 ) for 
his submission inat the order of dismissal can be issued only 
by the Manager and not by any other authority. S . U . NO. 
20 deals with the subject of termination of service while 
5 . 0 . NO. 29 deals with the rules of conduct of disciplinary 
iction . A bare reading of these two Standing Orders toge . 
ther makes it clear that they deal with entirely different sub 
jects. S .O . No. 29 deals with disciplinary action ; S . O . 26 
deals with termination of service simpliciter . The present 
workinan s case falls under S . O . 29 and not S . O . 26 . Refe : 
ance to S . O . 26 . , lic . etore , nui relevant, 


11 . The workman further claims justification for 70 days

in Dr. Warrier s Vaidyasliúin a ! Kottakkal in Kerala and that 
he svas further recommended rest for 35 days. It appears 
that a certificate was filed in support of this claim . The in 
quiry oíficar did not act upon this certificate , because in the 
first place it was not counters gned by the Medical Officer 
of the Management and secondly it purported to justify ab 
sence for just a small fraction of the total period of absence , 
which rúas into years. The view taken by the Enquiry Officer 
Cirinot te silid . to be unreasonable . 


8 . S . O . 29 ,6 ) no doubt provides inter alia tbat in awarding 
runishment, the Manager shall take into account the gravity 
of the inisconduct, the previous record of the workman , if 
any , and other cxtenuating and aggravating circumstances. 
from this, the workman desires the Tribunal to draw the 
inference that it is the Manager alone and no other authority 
shat can pass an order of dismissal. I do not agree . There 
is a schedule at the end of the Standing Orders . which de 
tails ihe authorities competent to pass orders of punishinents 
in disciplinary proceedings and authorities to whom appeals 
against the orders passed by the original authorities will 
lie . The original schedule was substituted by a fresh one 
under the Notification of 1st November , 1974 . Admittedly 
it applies to Factory workers and as such to the present 
workman . This schedule makes it clear that the Deputy 
Controiler, Ground Support, is the disciplinary authority who 
is en powered to pass an order of dismissal and Regional 
Director is the appellate authority . Miss Seth for the Man 
agement has stated categorically that the post of Deputy 
Director, Ground Support , is equivalent to that of Manager 
, and that Regional Director is still a superior officer , The 
use of the term Manager in S . O . 29 (6 ) is perhaps not happy, 
as on the first flush , it gives the impression that Manager? 
alone is enjoined to take into account relevant aspects before 
deciding upon the quantum of punishment. Now if the sub 
mission of the workman is accepted that the final order can 
be passed by the Manager alone, it will lead to some cbvi 
ously absurd consequences. The entire schedule drawn in de 
tail will become redundant. Then while disciplinary proceed 
ings will be conducted by officers of equal status as the 
Manager and appeals will be heard by still superior officers , 
the final orders will be in the hands of the Manager . It 
cannot be contemplated for a moment that such absurd results 
were intended by the corporation who have framed the Stand 
ing Orders under section 45 of the Air Corporations Act, 
with previous approval of the Central Government . Obviously 
in the context obtaining , the term Manager used in so . 
No. 29 (6 ) is loosely used for the term competent authority 
and cannot be assigned the special meaning given to it in the 
definition clause. 


12 . The findings of the Enquiry Officer were accepted by 
the Deputy Controller, Ground Support. He i sued showcause 
notice ( Exn . M - 5 ) to the workman on 7th January , 1985 as 
to why he should not be dismissed from service . The work 
man submitted his reply on 11th . There is para 4 he ex 
pressly agreed with the findings and prayed for mercy . There 
after he preferied an appeal to the Regional Director by his 
letter dated 11th March , 1985 (Exh . M - 8 ). It was here for 
the first time that he enclosed a photostat copy of a letter 
dated 281 February , 1984 addressed by one Dr. D Souza to 
une Dr, Mitra who is the Chief Medical Officer of the Cor 
poration . The first page of this letter only is filed on the 
record . It recites that the workman "vas admitted to Dr. 
D Souza Nursing Home on 29th December , 1983 with history 
of paranoid delusions, sleeplessness etc . It is rertinent to 
note that not a whisper of this letter is available in the 
enquiry proceedings anywhere . In these circumstances the ap 
pellatc authority cannot be faulted for not accepting the 
workman s belated , submissions. The finding in the enquiry 
that the workman remained absent for long spells of days 
witho rt sufficient grounds is entirely justified . 

13 . The last question concerns the quantum of punishment. 
Taking into consideration all the circumstances of the case , 
including the fact that the workman had worked for 24 vears 
in the Indian Air Force and had some domectic difficulties 
and is iequired to maintain a family of 5 members , I think 
the extreme penalty of dismissal is rather too harsh . T reduce 
it to removal from service simpliciter. 


14. The enquiry proceedings are held to be valid in all 
ir spects. The woman is tightly held guilty for misconduct 
ac defined in items 13 and 15 of S . O . 28 . "The penalty of 
lisarissal awarder to him is however reduced to removal 
from service simpliciter. Parties to bear their costs as incur 
red . Award accordingly . 

S . N . KHATRI, Presiding Officer 
[No. L -11012 / 2 /87- D . 11 (B ) / D . III(B ) ] 


9 . The impugned order cannot be faulted on the ground 
that it is not passed by a competent authority . [ uphold the 
competence of the Deputy Controller , Ground Support, to 
rass it and of the Regional Director to hear the appeal from 
it.
